  1. **`l3_large_ofi` (`step2_market_analysis.py` `analyze_market` & `analyze_market_served` + `SignalEngine`, *Cont, Kukanov & Stoikov 2014*):** Fixed the root order-of-operations bug where `l3.process_order_event(ev)` ran **before** `l3.update_order_book(...)` (`_ref_mid == 0.0 -> _near_weight() == 1.0` at `$20` away!); now initializes `_ref_mid` from live book/mid first so `near_touch_ofi` strictly measures `<= $2.50` orders (`>= 3` events, `|OFI| >= 25`), aligned with aggressive flow + `TREND` seatbelt.
  2. **`value_area` (`SignalEngine`, *Steidlmayer 1986; Dalton 2013 Mind Over Markets*):** Eliminated single-cycle double-voting and blind waterfall fading; in `TREND` votes initiative breakouts outside `VAH/VAL` only (never fading `near_vah`/`near_val`); in `RANGE`/`NEUTRAL` votes responsive mean-reversion tests of `VAH/VAL` back toward `POC` only.
  3. **`vwap_bands` (`SignalEngine`, *Institutional Session VWAP*):** Gated `±1σ` (`RANGE`) and `±2σ` (`regime != "TREND"`) out of `TREND` waterfalls; `±2.5σ` in `TREND` votes only on a true climax rejection candle (`CLV <= 0.35` / `>= 0.65`).
  4. **`cvd_divergence` (`SignalEngine`, *Easley, López de Prado & O'Hara 2012*):** Requires `|divergence| >= 0.35`, active-bar price rejection confirmation (filtering retail dip-buying into passive absorption during waterfalls), and `TREND`+`MTF` seatbelt.
  5. **`volume_roc` (`SignalEngine` & `tools/raw_replay.py`, *Wyckoff's 3rd Law: Effort vs. Result; Coulling 2013 VPA*):** Replaced blind 1-bar `vol[-1]/vol[-2]` chasing with 20-bar `RVOL >= 1.8x` separating **(a) Climax / Stopping Volume absorption reversals** (`pushed_hi + CLV <= 0.35 -> SELL`; `pushed_lo + CLV >= 0.65 -> BUY`) from **(b) Unstretched `TREND`+`MTF` breakout initiation** (`CLV >= 0.75 / <= 0.25`).
  6. **`absorption` & `iceberg` (`OrderBookDepthAnalyzer` & `SignalEngine`, *Frey & Sandås 2009 / QJF 2017; Cont & Kukanov 2017*):** `_prune_absorption` tracks `wall_px` and immediately invalidates broken passive walls once `best_bid < wall_px - 0.25` or `best_ask > wall_px + 0.25`, requiring `|absorption_net| >= 2` + `TREND` seatbelt; `iceberg` restricts to fresh (`last_seen <= 180s`), tight (`<= max(1.0*ATR, $1.50)`), unbroken walls confirmed by holding delta + `TREND` seatbelt.
  7. **`footprint_levels`, `footprint_delta`, `l3_imbalance`, `l3_aggr_limit`, `cvd_momentum`, `sweep` (`config.py` `0.03`/`1.5` + ATR-adaptive wick snapback + `TREND` seatbelt), plus bench judges `_detect_order_blocks` (`supply_demand` unmitigated impulse zones), `whale_walls` (live resting book only, zero `BID_CANCEL` bug), and `htf_poc` (Dalton `H1/H4` value migration vs `H4` balance magnet):** All healed in `step2_market_analysis.py` with synced weight/note patterns across `judge_panel.py` & `audit_day.py`.

  5. **`cvd_momentum` & `l3_large_ofi` (`Bouchaud 2018; Easley, López de Prado & O'Hara 2012; Cartea et al. 2015`):** Scales `cvd_momentum` dynamically by active bar window (`Law #11`: `35*tf_scale` lots, `>=60%` pressure, `CLV` in winning `40%`, anchor-HTF seatbelt) and requires real aggressive tape follow-through (`>=4` near-touch large adds, `|near_OFI|>=40`, `|aggr_net|>=15`, bar + anchor-HTF alignment) in `l3_large_ofi`.

  1. **`SignalEngine.news_sentiment_ramp` + `config.NEWS_SENTIMENT_DEADBAND=0.15` / `NEWS_SENTIMENT_FULL_WEIGHT_AT=0.60`:** scores `|s| <= 0.15` cast **no vote** (`0.0`), above that the applied weight ramps linearly to full at `0.60` (`+0.30 -> w 0.33`, `+0.65 -> w 1.00`); a silent micro-score now logs `news sentiment +0.02 inside dead-band +-0.15 -> silent (no vote)` (deliberately **not** matching the `HIGH impact sentiment` panel regex, so the panel reflects the true vote).

## Check-in stamp — `2026-11-09 17:10 CET` Section `#12` `Stage N2` (News-Perimeter Alignment + Calendar Hygiene) Installed — the last item that could wrongly BLOCK live trading is gone

- **Defect (3) healed — HIGH boost window reads the NEAREST HIGH event (`step2_market_analysis.py`):** `_high_now` used to pair `EconomicCalendar.classify()`'s impact level (the **most important event of the whole week**) with `news_state()`'s `minutes_to_next_event` (the **nearest gateable event of any impact**, LOW skipped by `NEWS_PERIMETER_IGNORE_LOW`). A `HIGH` event `300` min away next to a `MEDIUM` event `5` min away therefore opened a HIGH window, boosting `macro_risk`/`news_sentiment` weights to full on fiction. New `EconomicCalendar.nearest_high_event()` returns the **signed minutes to the nearest HIGH event**, exposed as `NewsAndEvents.high_impact_minutes` / `high_impact_title`; `_high_now` prefers it and only falls back to the legacy `impact_level + minutes_to_next_event` pair when the snapshot does not supply it (so every existing caller/test keeps its exact behaviour). `classify()` stays display-only.
- **Defect (4) healed — calendar hygiene, no more fabricated events:** `EconomicCalendar._read/_write_cached_events` hard-coded `Path(__file__).parent / "data"` (silently escaping `conftest`/replay isolation and writing into the live repo during tests); they now resolve `config.DATA_DIR` through `_data_dir()` / `_cache_path()`. And `get_upcoming_events()` no longer substitutes `fallback_events()`' **invented FOMC/CPI/NFP dates**: an empty feed now returns an EMPTY calendar so the perimeter stays `QUIET`, because a pure feed outage could otherwise manufacture a real `BLACKOUT` window (`10` min before the fake FOMC date) and block perfectly good trades. Opt-in for any future simulation: `NEWS_ALLOW_FAKE_EVENTS=1` (default `0`).
- **Cadence self-check (defect (5) observation, no grid change):** `[8e]` line 1 now ends with `Bell_Share=X% (target ~33.3% for a 60s cadence on M3)` — the explicit replacement for a fragile implicit assumption. The cycle grid itself is **deliberately unchanged** (changing it would break comparability with `replay-28..30` for a purely cosmetic alignment gain; `replay-30`'s `Bell(:00)=995` vs `Serve(:01/:02)=1,977` already proves the `1:2` Bell/Serve structure).
- **Evidence:** `test_nov09_stage_n2_news_perimeter.py` (`13` tests: nearest-HIGH selection + none-case, the exact `300-vs-5` bug, the legacy path reproducing the old boost, near-HIGH still boosting, just-released HIGH inside the window, `DATA_DIR` cache round-trip in `tmp_path` with no repo writes, empty calendar -> `[]`, opt-in placeholders, empty calendar cannot manufacture a `BLACKOUT` while the legacy placeholders provably do, config defaults, snapshot defaults) -> **`556 passed, 3 subtests passed`** (`M5`) and **`55 passed`** on the simulated Windows `M3` `.env`; zero existing test altered.
- **Live-trading impact (the reason this came before `#13/#14`):** with `Stage N2` a calendar/RSS outage can only ever make the robot **quieter** (no invented blackouts), never silently open a boosted `WORLD` window from a distant high-impact event.
- **Next:** `replay-31` (must keep `[8c]`/`[8d]`/`[8h]`/`[8i]` identical on unchanged tape + show the new `Bell_Share` tail) -> then Sections `#13/#14` (Step 3/4 + Evening Audit) to finish `14/14`.

## Check-in stamp — `2026-11-09 18:20 CET` Section `#13` (Step 3 Doorman) Manager Check & Isolated `Stage D1` Parse + Cache Hardening Installed

- **Pass 1–3 findings on `step3_ai_decision.py` (1,506 lines, read line-by-line):** three live defects, all in the *plumbing* rather than in the AI's judgement — (1) **free-prose action guessing:** `_parse_response` fell back to `if "BUY" in text.upper()` and scraped confidence from the **first** `\d{1,3}%` in the text, so a refusal ("I would NOT buy here"), an error string, or a commentary line containing the word `buy` plus a stray percentage could produce an **unvalidated `BUY` at an arbitrary confidence** — only partially contained by the Doorman meta-gate (which vetoes *conflicting* sides but cannot see a wrong-same-side call); (2) **blind cache key:** `_prompt_hash` hashed only `price/direction/strength/regime/news_state`, ignoring `impact_level`, `minutes_to_next_event`, `high_impact_minutes`, `sentiment_score` and `signal_confidence`, so a decision cached in a `QUIET` cycle could be re-used inside a `WARNING` window or seconds before a HIGH event (`AI_CACHE_MINUTES=10`); (3) **shared cached object:** `_check_cache` returned the *same* `Decision` instance it stored, so any downstream mutation silently rewrote the cache for later cycles.
- **`Stage D1` installed (Doorman layer only — zero judge/gate/exit changes):**
  1. **`AIDecisionEngine._extract_json_object` + fail-closed `_parse_response`:** strict `json.loads` first, then a **balanced-brace scan** (the old greedy `\{.*\}` match swallowed prose and two objects at once); **no JSON -> `HOLD @ 0.0%`** with `unparseable AI response -> fail-closed HOLD`. Legacy behaviour is retained behind `AI_PARSE_KEYWORD_FALLBACK=1`, and even then a keyword-derived `BUY`/`SELL` requires an **explicit** `confidence: N` mention of at least `AI_PARSE_MIN_KEYWORD_CONF=60` — a bare percentage in prose no longer counts.
  2. **`_prompt_hash` v2 (`AI_CACHE_KEY_V2=1`):** adds `signal_confidence`, `impact_level`, 5-minute buckets of `minutes_to_next_event` and `high_impact_minutes`, and `round(sentiment*20)`, so cache hits can only happen inside the same bar-scale news/edge state. `AI_CACHE_KEY_V2=0` restores the legacy 5-field key.
  3. **`_check_cache` returns `copy.copy(decision)`** — callers can no longer mutate the cached entry (the `-cached` model relabel stays honest and isolated).
  4. **Untouched by design (verified by test):** `AI_ALLOW_SYNTHETIC_FALLBACK=0` / `AI_FALLBACK_ENABLED=0` (fail-closed `HOLD` stays canonical), `AI_DOORMAN_META_GATE_ENABLED=1` (primary engine owns the side; the Doorman may only veto to `HOLD`, never flip `BUY<->SELL`), the single-budget cascade + key cooldown + model-health ordering, and `build_prompt < 15,000` chars.
- **Evidence:** `test_nov09_stage_d1_doorman_hardening.py` (`19` tests: prose-with-`buy` -> `HOLD`, JSON wins, JSON-in-prose, two-objects-first-valid, broken/empty/`429` -> `HOLD`, legacy opt-in with explicit confidence, bare percentage ignored, low confidence -> `HOLD`, `_sanitize` clamps, cache-key changes with perimeter/confidence, key stability, legacy key toggle, cache-hit copy independence, window expiry, fail-closed config defaults, meta-gate veto-only invariant) -> **`575 passed, 3 subtests passed`** (`M5`) and **`53 passed`** on the simulated Windows `M3` `.env`; zero existing test altered.
- **Owner-`.env` finding (recorded, not changed — `.env` is owner-managed):** the first live run failed `test_d1_fail_closed_defaults_unchanged` because the live `.env` carries **`AI_FALLBACK_ENABLED=1`**. The assertion was wrong, not the code: `decide()` only enters the synthetic fallback when the **master** switch `AI_ALLOW_SYNTHETIC_FALLBACK` is on (`step3_ai_decision.py:1475`), and `AI_FALLBACK_ENABLED` is only the inner guard `AND`ed inside `_fallback_decision` (`:913`). The test now asserts the **conjunction** invariant (`not (AI_FALLBACK_ENABLED and AI_ALLOW_SYNTHETIC_FALLBACK)`) plus a behavioural test that `_fallback_decision()` returns `None` while the master switch is off, and it was re-run **with `AI_FALLBACK_ENABLED=1`** to reproduce the owner's machine. **Latent note:** with `AI_FALLBACK_ENABLED=1` sitting in `.env`, flipping `AI_ALLOW_SYNTHETIC_FALLBACK=1` would switch the Doorman from fail-closed `HOLD` to a **rule-based synthetic `BUY`/`SELL`** — the owner's call, but it is one line away.
- **Next:** `replay-32` (Doorman changes are AI-path only — offline replay has no Gemini, so `[8c]`/`[8d]`/`[8h]`/`[8i]`/`[8j]`/`[8k]` must be unchanged on the same tape) -> then Section `#14` (Step 4 MT5 Executor) + the Evening Audit to reach `14/14`.

## SEAL — `2026-11-09` Section `#13` STEP 3 AI DOORMAN (`step3_ai_decision.py`) — `13/14` DONE

**Manager check scope (Four-Pass Audit, `Law #6/#7/#8/#9`):** full line-by-line read of `step3_ai_decision.py` (`1,506` lines): the `decide()` cascade (`:1164-1450` — total wait budget, per-attempt slices, key cooldown, model-health ordering, L3 confidence calibration, Doorman meta-gate), `_parse_response`/`_sanitize` (`:1472-1506`), `_check_cache` / `_prompt_hash` / `_update_cache` / `_fallback_decision`, and `build_prompt` (`:623-840`, the `G-03..G-06` compression contract).

### Three live defects found (all in the *plumbing*, none in the AI's judgement)
1. **Free-prose action guessing** — `_parse_response` fell back to `if "BUY" in text.upper()` and scraped confidence from the **first** `\d{1,3}%` in the text. A refusal (*"I would NOT buy here"*), an error string, or a commentary line containing `buy` plus a stray percentage could produce an **unvalidated `BUY` at an arbitrary confidence**. The Doorman meta-gate cannot catch it: the gate vetoes *conflicting* sides, so a wrong **same-side** call sails through.
2. **Blind cache key** — `_prompt_hash` hashed only `price / direction / strength / regime / news_state`, ignoring `impact_level`, `minutes_to_next_event`, `high_impact_minutes`, `sentiment_score` and `signal_confidence`, while `AI_CACHE_MINUTES=10` allows up to `3` consecutive `M3` cycles to reuse one answer. A `QUIET`-cycle decision could be replayed inside a `WARNING` window, after a headline flip (`0.00 -> -0.60`), or as a HIGH event closes from `240m` to `60m`. (`news_state` alone already covered the `WARNING/BLACKOUT` transition — the real leak is sentiment / minutes-to-event / nearest-HIGH-window / confidence.)
3. **Shared cached object** — `_check_cache` returned the **same** `Decision` instance it stored, so any downstream mutation silently rewrote the cache for later cycles.

### What was installed (Doorman layer only — zero judge / gate / exit / threshold / `.env` change)
- **`Stage D1` (`a6d42ab`)** — `AIDecisionEngine._extract_json_object` (strict `json.loads`, then a **balanced-brace scan**; the old greedy `\{.*\}` swallowed prose and two objects at once) + **fail-closed** `_parse_response` (**no JSON -> `HOLD @ 0.0`**, *"unparseable AI response -> fail-closed HOLD"*); legacy keyword path only behind `AI_PARSE_KEYWORD_FALLBACK=1` and even then requires an **explicit** `confidence\D{0,12}(\d{1,3})` of at least `AI_PARSE_MIN_KEYWORD_CONF=60`; **`_prompt_hash` v2** (`AI_CACHE_KEY_V2=1`) = `price(.1f) | direction | strength(.0f) | regime | news_state | impact_level | round(mte/5) | round(high_impact_minutes/5) or "-" | round(sentiment*20) | round(signal_confidence)`; `_check_cache` returns `copy.copy(decision)`.
- **`Stage D1a` (test-only)** — the first live run failed one assertion because the owner's `.env` carries **`AI_FALLBACK_ENABLED=1`**. The **assertion** was wrong, not the code: `decide()` enters the synthetic fallback only via the **master** switch `AI_ALLOW_SYNTHETIC_FALLBACK` (`:1475`), and `AI_FALLBACK_ENABLED` is just the inner guard `AND`ed inside `_fallback_decision` (`:913`). The test now asserts the **conjunction** invariant plus a behavioural test (`_fallback_decision() is None` while the master switch is off) and was re-run with `AI_FALLBACK_ENABLED=1` to reproduce the owner's machine. *(Latent note, owner's call: with `AI_FALLBACK_ENABLED=1` sitting in `.env`, flipping `AI_ALLOW_SYNTHETIC_FALLBACK=1` alone would turn fail-closed `HOLD` into a rule-based synthetic `BUY/SELL`.)*
- **`Stage D2` (`b6e79b0`)** — owner correction accepted (*"our robot is a scalper on the 3 minute chart ... we only need the last 3-4 candles"*): the Doorman was reading `20` bars = **`60` minutes** of `M3` history (`.env:56 AI_MAX_PROMPT_BARS=20`) while its own grading clocks run `+3/+6/+9/+12` min. Now `AI_PROMPT_BARS` (new preferred name, legacy name still honoured) default **`4`** with floor **`3`** in both clamps (`config.py`, `step2_market_analysis.py:7587`) and default `4` in the step-3 trim (`step3_ai_decision.py:639`): **`4` bars = `12` min = the `+12` min grading clock**. The 15/20-bar block was also largely redundant with the full-dataframe indicator blocks (`SMA 9/20/50`, `EMA`, `ADX/±DI`, `MACD`, `ATR/BB/RSI`, `power`, `regime`, `mtf_trends`) and sat at payload field `42/112` (mid-context, *Lost in the Middle*, Liu et al. 2023).

## Check-in stamp — `2026-11-09` Section `#14` (Step 4 MT5 Executor) Manager Check + Isolated `Stage X1` Fill-Identity Hardening

- **Pass 1-2 findings on `step4_mt5_execution.py` (`1,452` lines, read end-to-end):** the ten-lock gate ladder before `order_send()` is genuinely strong (pipeline permit, DEMO-order smoke marker, `TRADING_ENABLED`, `EXECUTION_MODE=python`, empty-snapshot block, confidence, direction, news `BLACKOUT`, MT5 availability, **REAL-account refusal** when `ALLOW_LIVE_TRADING=0`, cost guard, real-risk guard). The defect sits **after** the send, in *proving what happened*: (1) **dead fill identity** -- `_wait_for_bot_position` contained `if order_id is None or ticket == order_id or positions:` where `or positions:` is always truthy, so the first bot position was returned regardless of identity, and it compared an **order** ticket with a **position** ticket (three different integers in MT5: `order.ticket`, `deal.ticket`, `position.ticket`). `_place_order` then wrote that ticket into `trade_history.record_entry()` / `record_tca()`, so a stale or unrelated position could be remembered as this entry -> **every downstream scorecard (`[8c]`/`[8d]`/`[8j]`) inherits a false attribution**; (2) **binary retcode taxonomy** -- only `10009 DONE` counts, so `10010 DONE_PARTIAL` (money IS live) is reported as `ERROR` with no memory write, and `10004 REQUOTE` / `10020 PRICE_CHANGED` / `10021 PRICE_OFF` get no bounded retry (deferred to a later stage); (3) `order_send()` is unwrapped (an exception escapes into the loop with a possible live order) and no `request_id` idempotency key is sent; (4) `_close_position` returns `"closed"` on `DONE`/`DONE_PARTIAL` without re-checking `positions_get`, while `_place_order` immediately opens the opposite side (hedging accounts can hold both sides).
- **`Stage X1` installed (fill identity only -- no gate, threshold, sizing, bracket, close or retcode behaviour touched):**
  1. **`MT5Executor._position_id_from_deal()`** -- resolves the executed **deal** (`history_deals_get(ticket=...)`, then `position=...`) to its `position_id`, the authoritative identity of the trade we just opened.
  2. **`_wait_for_bot_position(order_id, timeout, *, deal_id, expect_volume, not_before_epoch)`** -- resolution order: deal -> `position_id`; else position ticket == order ticket; else (only when **no** identity is available) exactly **one** position opened at/after `not_before_epoch` whose volume matches `expect_volume`. Two or more candidates -> `None` + a `WARNING` ("refusing to guess"). A **known** identity that does not match is treated as "not yet visible" (poll, then `None`) -- the search is never widened to a bystander.
  3. **Both call sites pass the identity** -- B3 limit path (`order_id`, `deal_id`, `expect_volume=lot_size`, `not_before_epoch=now-2s`) and the market path (same, plus `deal_id`), so `record_entry` / `record_tca` / `ExecutionResult.position_id` all carry the **verified** ticket.
- **Fail-closed semantics:** an unidentifiable or ambiguous fill now returns `None` -> the caller reports `ERROR`/`DEFERRED` and writes **nothing** to trade memory, instead of memoising a guess.
- **Evidence:** `test_nov09_stage_x1_executor_identity.py` (`23` tests with a fake MT5 SDK: bystander never returned; deal identity beats an older bystander; order-ticket fallback for netting brokers; `history_deals_get` raising falls back safely; single new matching position accepted; volume mismatch rejected; position opened before the cycle rejected; two ambiguous candidates refuse to guess + `WARNING` logged; foreign `magic` untouched; `_position_id_from_deal` corners -- `None`/`0`/negative/`""`/`"abc"`/`"9001x"`/missing `position_id`/no SDK; `positions_get -> None` does not raise; source guards that the always-true expression is gone, both call sites pass identity, and the **verified** ticket is what gets recorded and returned) -> **`611 passed, 3 subtests passed`** (`M5`) and **`168 passed`** on the simulated Windows `M3` profile (executor-adjacent suites included); zero existing test altered.
- **`Stage X1` VERIFIED on `replay-34`** (generated `2026-11-09 23:32:25 CET`, `3,374` cyc / `3,169` SIG): `[8b]`, `[8c]` (`N=36`, `12/24 33.3% -188.3`, Stage-4b `16/19 45.7% -168.9`), `[8d]` (`9` trades `5/4 55.6% -21.8`, per-day `1/0/3/2` = `-12.5/0.0/-14.4/-12.3`), `[8h]` (`STOP=5 TARGET=2 TIME=5`), `[8j]` (`9` trades, `HALT_S 3/5`) are **byte-IDENTICAL** to `replay-33` even though `11-09` grew `680 -> 795` cycles -- the extra 115 cycles added **no new GO entries** and re-priced the same brackets identically. `[8i]`/`[8k]` differ only in cycle denominators (`crossed` still exactly `9`, same `18:53` sample), and `[8e] Bell_Share=33.5%`. Step 4 is MT5-only, so a zero-drift replay is the correct expected outcome.
- **Next:** `Stage X2` (retcode taxonomy: `DONE_PARTIAL` as live, bounded requote retry) and `Stage X3` (`order_send` exception containment + `request_id` idempotency), then the Evening Audit for `14/14`.

## Check-in stamp — `2026-11-09` Section `#14` Isolated `Stage X2` (broker-agnostic retcode taxonomy)

- **Owner constraint recorded:** he does not know (and may change) his MT5 account type -- **demo now, possibly live later, different MT5 platforms / CFD brokers, hedging or netting**. Design consequence: the send path must be **account-type agnostic**. MT5 `retcode`s are identical across platforms and brokers, so X2 branches on retcodes only and **never reads `margin_mode` / `ACCOUNT_MARGIN_MODE`** (asserted by test).
- **Defect X2 (Section `#14` manager check):** `_place_order` accepted exactly one outcome -- `10009 DONE`. Therefore (a) **`10010 DONE_PARTIAL`**, where the broker filled less than requested and **money IS live**, was reported as `ERROR` with no `record_entry`, no trade memory and no position bookkeeping -- the robot believed nothing happened while holding a position; (b) `10004 REQUOTE` / `10020 PRICE_CHANGED` / `10021 PRICE_OFF` -- transient broker price races that normally succeed on a refreshed tick (routine on a fast gold tape) -- were terminal, with no retry; (c) `order_send()` was unwrapped, so an exception escaped into the loop.
- **`Stage X2` installed (send-path only -- no gate, sizing, bracket, close or panel behaviour touched):**
  1. **`MT5Executor._retcode_taxonomy()`** -- one stable bucket per code: `OK 10009`, `PARTIAL 10010`, `PLACED 10008`, `RETRYABLE {10004,10020,10021}`, `FATAL {10006,10007,10013,10014,10015,10016,10018,10019}`, `UNKNOWN`, `NONE`.
  2. **`MT5Executor._send_market_order()`** -- bounded retry (default `MT5_RETRY_MAX=2`) for the `RETRYABLE` family **and** for a raised/empty send: each attempt fetches a **fresh tick**, re-prices the order and **re-derives the SL/TP offsets** so a retried order never carries the stale bracket the broker just rejected; the retry loop is budget-bounded so a dead IPC can never spin; `PARTIAL` returns immediately and is **never re-sent** (a live fill must not be doubled).
  3. **`PARTIAL` is treated as a LIVE fill** -- it now flows into the X1 identity verification (`deal -> position_id`) and gets recorded, instead of being discarded by a second `retcode == DONE` check (that check was removed from the market path; it remains, correctly, on the pending-limit path where `DONE` means *placed*).
  4. **`request_id`** is attached before the send (dedupe key for platforms that honour it) and **reused across retries**.
  5. **`deviation`** is no longer hardcoded (`20` -> `config.MT5_DEVIATION`, clamped `1..200`) -- some brokers reject on tighter slippage tolerance.
  6. **X1 hysteresis tightened:** the positional fallback can only adopt a position of **EXACTLY the requested volume**; a grown/merged position (possible on a netting account) is never silently adopted as this entry.
- **Evidence:** `test_nov09_stage_x2_executor_retcode_taxonomy.py` (`34` tests: 13-code taxonomy table + `RETRYABLE`/`FATAL` disjointness; `PARTIAL` reported live and **not** re-sent; `10004/10020/10021` retried to success; retry uses the **refreshed tick price**; retry **re-derives SL/TP distances**; retry budget honoured and `MT5_RETRY_MAX=0` disables it; fatal codes never retried; a raising send is returned not raised and stops inside budget; a missing tick on retry stops cleanly; source guards that the DONE-only market check is gone, `PARTIAL` is logged as live, `request_id` precedes the send, `deviation` comes from config, **`margin_mode` never appears in the module**, and the exact-volume rule is documented) -> **`645 passed, 3 subtests passed`** (`M5`) and **`145 passed`** on the owner's simulated Windows `M3` subset (`X1` + `X2` + `demo_order_smoke` + `main_shooting_execution_gate` + `market_quality_guards`); also re-run green with `MT5_RETRY_MAX=0`. Zero existing test altered. **Owner install receipt (`2026-11-09`): `ddc5627`** -- patch `1310d72b6ed7bd5959584877bd9ccaf35dbac348c0235bc5c71f3f7077629547`, `4` files `+462/-12`, Book `1717 -> 1734` lines, tests `34` / `645` / `145`, `.env` untouched.
- **Next:** `replay-35` (Step 4 is MT5-only -> must be **identical** to `replay-34`) -> then `Stage X3` (`order_send` exception containment is now partially covered by X2; X3 = full `_close_position` verification + explicit `NO_MONEY`/`INVALID_STOPS` operator messaging), then the Evening Audit for `14/14`.

## Standing working laws added `2026-11-10` (owner instructions + operational findings)

1. **WORKING LANGUAGE (owner, `2026-11-10`):** every report, answer and summary is written in **English**. No Hungarian or other-language summary blocks, in this or any future check-in.
2. **A DEAD-FEED STATE IS A DESIGNED STATE, NOT A FAULT (verified from the live log `2026-11-09 23:58`, BookMap closed, loop running):** five independent fail-closed layers fire in order -- `bookmap_bridge_provider` (`quote=unavailable`, `L2 suppressed`, `MBO=0`, `feed age 3527s`) -> POWER (`NEITHER`, `act 0.00`, `regime_gate=FAIL`, `safety_gate=FAIL`, `reason=STALE_FEED`) -> `stage3r_gate` (`mbo_feed_unavailable`, blind-depth entry blocked) -> Step 3 (`HOLD`, `signal 0.0 < 6`, AI skipped, no Gemini call) -> `SAFETY: SESSION` (`CLOSED`, plus `BUDAPEST OFF-HOURS`) -> Step 4 (`SKIPPED`). Running the loop against a dead feed is safe and expected: nothing can reach `order_send()`.
3. **NEVER STOP BOOKMAP / THE PC WHILE A POSITION IS OPEN (permanent safety rule):** with a dead feed, Step 5 monitoring, the PM trails, the Escort and every exit read a frozen price and cannot protect the position. Check `data/pm_state.json` before any restart -- only `_flow` means flat and safe. Owner follows this; it is now law.
4. **CLOSED-MARKET ARTIFACT FOOTPRINT IS BOUNDED (receipts):** `decisions_log.csv` trimmed to `DECISIONS_LOG_MAX_ROWS=5000` (`maintenance.trim_csvs`); `snapshots_history.jsonl` rotates at `>50 MB` (`main.py:1125`); `logs/trading_*.log` deleted after `LOG_RETENTION_DAYS` (`maintenance.rotate_logs`); `market_snapshot.json` overwritten per cycle. The one unbounded-by-design file is the per-day mirror `data/decisions_YYYYMMDD.csv` (`24k A2`, `main.py:988`): a night of closed-market cycles adds `NEUTRAL 0.0` rows that `audit_day.py:1803` classifies as **"SKIPPED before the executor"** -- honest noise in a count, never a fake signal.
5. **VERIFICATION REPLAYS ARE PINNED BY DATE, NOT DISCOVERED (`2026-11-10`):** `tools/raw_replay.py --days N` (default `11`, `:4293`) auto-discovers the newest raw folders, so the moment BookMap writes a new day the window silently shifts (`11-03..11-09` -> `11-04..11-10`) and every scorecard moves because the DAYS changed, not the code. Witness replays must pin the days: `--dates 2026-11-03,2026-11-04,2026-11-05,2026-11-06,2026-11-09` (`:4290`). `--days N` stays for exploratory runs only.
6. **PATCH DELIVERY LAW (my process failure, recorded so it cannot repeat):** a patch is presentable only after `git apply --check` **and** `git apply` + `cmp` per file inside a clean worktree of a commit the owner actually has. My shadow branch is not evidence. A Book edit made after a patch ships rides only inside the next patch generated from the owner's base -- never as an undelivered standalone commit. Every Book file keeps LF endings, no trailing blank line at EOF, and its expected `wc -l` is stated before the owner applies. **Anchor rule added `2026-11-10` (this failure happened again):** the base commit for a patch is **the commit whose `HOLY_BOOK.md` blob equals the blob on the owner's `origin` HEAD** -- verify with `git rev-parse <owner_head>:HOLY_BOOK.md` and match it against a shadow commit, never assume "the last code-shipping commit". A Book patch applied after another Book patch moves the blob, so `git diff <old-code-commit> HEAD` will fail at `HOLY_BOOK.md:<line>` even when every code hunk is fine (`git apply` is atomic -> nothing lands).

7. **ONE VERSION EVERYWHERE — WORKSPACE = OWNER'S PC = GITHUB (owner's instruction `2026-11-10`):** the app version is a **single fact**, never three. After every stage there must be exactly ONE commit that is simultaneously (a) the head of my shadow workspace, (b) `HEAD` of the owner's `/a/gitHub/Gold-BookMap` working tree, and (c) `origin/shadow-only-review` on `https://github.com/jafarimahdi/Gold-BookMap`. A stage is not "installed" until all three agree; a Book edit is not "done" until it is in the next patch (delivery law `§6`). **Verification is by content hash, not by eyeball:** the blob SHA (`git rev-parse <commit>:<path>`, i.e. `git hash-object <path>`) of every changed file must be identical in all three places -- blob SHAs are content hashes, so equal SHA = byte-identical file, LF endings included. **I verify the GitHub side myself** from the public API (`https://api.github.com/repos/jafarimahdi/Gold-BookMap/commits/shadow-only-review`) at every check-in and quote the remote `sha`, `tree`, `stats` and per-file blob SHAs in the report; the owner verifies his own side with `git log --oneline -1` + `git status -sb` + `git diff origin/shadow-only-review --stat` (must be empty). Divergence is a **stop-the-line** condition: never patch, never replay, never start the loop on a version that is not identical in all three places.

8. **ONE NOTEBOOK — THE HOLY BOOK IS THE ONLY PLACE FOR NOTES (owner's instruction `2026-11-10`):** every change, receipt, audit finding, runbook, to-do list, x-ray, manager-checkup and status note lives **only in `HOLY_BOOK.md`**. No side `.md` files, no separate report files, no `TODO.md` / `STATUS.md` / `MORNING.md` — they get deleted and their content folded into the Book (the three such files created on `2026-11-10` were removed; their content is in the `Morning runbook`, `Tri-sync receipt` and `X-Ray & Manager Checkup` sections of this Book). The nightly deliverables remain what they always were: **one patch** plus, when asked, the replay `.txt` reports that `tools/raw_replay.py` itself writes. Rationale: one notebook cannot contradict itself, and a fact that lives in two files is a fact that will someday live in two versions.

## Check-in stamp — `2026-11-10` `Stage X2` VERIFIED on `replay-35` + Section `#14` `Stage X3` (verified close + operator messaging)

- **`Stage X2` VERIFIED on `replay-35`** (generated `2026-11-10 00:56:51 CET`, `3,374` cyc / `3,169` SIG, days `11-03..11-09` pinned): **every report block `[8]`, `[8b]`..`[8k]` is byte-identical to `replay-34`** -- cycles `629/739/784/427/795`, signals `15/9/30/21/18`, FIX `-59.2/+60.7/+4.1/-87.1/-18.5`, PM_TRAIL `-75.1/+7.0/-66.0/-34.7/+30.5`, `[8c] N=36`, `[8d] 9` trades `5/4`, `[8h] STOP=5 TARGET=2 TIME=5`, `[8j] HALT_S 3/5`, `[8e] Bell_Share=33.5%`, `[8k] HIGH_WINDOW=120m`. Only two lines differ in the whole file: the `Generated:` timestamp and the per-day **`SEC` wall-clock column** (`1622.5s -> 1242.0s` etc. -- `replay-34` was measured while BookMap and the loop were running, `replay-35` ran alone). `SEC` is a runtime measurement, not a market measurement, so it is **excluded from the Law #8 identity contract**; every market/strategy column matched exactly. Zero drift, exactly as predicted for an MT5-only stage.
- **Defect X3 (Section `#14` manager check):** `_close_position()` accepted `10009 DONE` / `10010 DONE_PARTIAL` as *proof* the trade was gone. `DONE` is the broker's receipt for the **request**: a close that is rejected, silently reduced (`DONE_PARTIAL` leaves the ticket OPEN at lower volume) or delayed by the terminal was still reported as `closed`, so Step 5, PM trails, the Escort and the operator all believed a **live position** did not exist -- the most dangerous lie this file can tell. Separately, the two rejections the operator can actually fix (`10019 NO_MONEY`, `10016 INVALID_STOPS`) reached the log and `data/decisions_YYYYMMDD.csv` as a bare `retcode=…` with no statement of whether money was opened and no instruction.
- **`Stage X3` (close-verification + operator messaging only -- no gate, threshold, sizing, bracket, retry or panel behaviour touched):**
  1. **`_position_remaining_volume(ticket)`** reads the position back with `positions_get()`: `0.0` = MT5 confirms gone, `>0` = still live at that volume, **`None` = unverifiable and therefore never "closed"** (`None` result, query exception, SDK without the call).
  2. **`_close_position()` verifies after every send** inside a bounded window (`MT5_CLOSE_VERIFY_SECONDS`, `0..15`s, default `3.0`; `0` = one immediate check, never zero checks): ticket gone -> `closed`; still open -> `OPERATOR ACTION — CLOSE NOT CONFIRMED … money is LIVE`; reduced volume -> `OPERATOR ACTION — PARTIAL CLOSE … X lots STILL LIVE`; unreadable -> `OPERATOR ACTION — close UNVERIFIED`. A close is **never re-sent** (a double close is its own disaster), and verified state beats the receipt: a rejected send whose ticket is gone is reported flat.
  3. **`_operator_message()`** turns every fatal rejection into one greppable `OPERATOR ACTION — <LABEL> (retcode N)` line: `NO_MONEY`, `INVALID_STOPS`, `REJECT`, `CANCEL`, `INVALID_REQUEST`, `INVALID_VOLUME`, `INVALID_PRICE`, `MARKET_CLOSED`, plus `SEND FAILED` for an empty result. Each states first whether a position was opened, then what the operator can do (`MAX_LOT_SIZE` / `RISK_PER_TRADE_PCT` / `STOP_LOSS_ATR_MULT`). The text rides in `ExecutionResult.reason`, so the operator's evening read of `decisions_YYYYMMDD.csv` shows it. The local margin pre-check uses the same `NO_MONEY` label.
  4. Owner portability preserved: retcode labels are platform/broker independent and **`margin_mode` is still never read** (asserted).

## Law §7 addendum — `2026-11-10` (what counts as "the app version"): `Workspace/*.bmw` is environment, not code

First morning check after the law was installed exposed the exact edge the law needs to define. Owner's `git status -sb` at `73e2245` showed **no ahead/behind and no code file changed**, but `git diff origin/... --stat` was **not empty**:

```
 M Workspace/26,09,16-23.20.bmw        (55 insertions, 149 deletions)
warning: in the working copy of 'Workspace/26,09,16-23.20.bmw', LF will be replaced by CRLF
```

Diagnosis (**verified, not assumed**): `Workspace/*.bmw` is a **BookMap chart workspace file** -- BookMap itself rewrites it whenever the chart layout/panes/saved state changes, in BookMap's own CRLF encoding. It is tracked in the repo for convenience but it is **environment state**, not app code. My side keeps the committed blob `dbeec50846dccafcbde064224f13006fd1251bb1` (`3,921` lines) untouched; the owner's copy differs only because BookMap has since re-saved the layout.

**Addendum (in force):** the "one version everywhere" check compares **the app** -- every tracked file **except** `Workspace/**` (BookMap chart state), `data/**` and `logs/**` (runtime output). Correct verification command:

```bash
git diff origin/shadow-only-review --stat -- . ':(exclude)Workspace' ':(exclude)data' ':(exclude)logs'
# EMPTY = app version identical in all three places
```

`Workspace/*.bmw` churn is expected and must **never be committed** as part of a stage patch (it would push one machine's chart layout onto another). The window's `LF will be replaced by CRLF` warning on that file is BookMap's own line endings, not a defect.

The dated replay chunks live under the repo (`data/` and `data/archive/`), which is exactly why every earlier witness replay worked from the repo root with `--days`/`--dates` and no extra flags. Therefore `--root-dir /a/gitHub/Gold-BookMap` finds them; the only case needing a different path is if a day's chunks sit outside the repo, and `ls data/archive | grep -E "^(ticks|mbo)_"` is the 5-second check. **Measured `2026-11-10`:** `ls data | grep -E "^(ticks|mbo)_"` = **`0`** while `ls data/archive | grep -E "^(ticks|mbo)_"` = **`218`** dated `.csv.gz` chunks (`ticks_20261103_143238.csv.gz`, ...) -- i.e. **the history lives in `data/archive/`, compressed, and `data/` itself holds only live/scratch files.** Any `ls data/ticks_YYYYMMDD_*` check is therefore meaningless on this rig; use `data/archive`.

**A/B replay mechanics (verified in code before handing the commands over -- `tools/raw_replay.py` + `config.py`):** a `git worktree` of an old commit is **not** a runnable replay by itself, for three reasons, all checked: (1) **`config.py` loads `.env` from `BASE_DIR` = the directory of `config.py`**, and `.env` is untracked, so a fresh worktree silently runs on **default** config (`TIMEFRAME=M5`, no `AI_PROMPT_BARS`, ...) -- the `.env` **must be copied into the worktree** or the A/B compares two different robots; (2) **raw `data/` is untracked**, so pass `--root-dir /a/gitHub/Gold-BookMap` to read the real raw CSVs (with a custom root-dir `use_env_paths=False`, so `BOOKMAP_BRIDGE_FILE` is ignored and the `root_dir`/`root_dir/data`/`.../archive` scan is what finds them) and `--data-dir <temp>` so the run writes **nothing** into the live repo; (3) the worktree has **no `.venv`**, so call the main repo's interpreter by absolute path. Verified by reading the code at those commits (`--date/--dates/--start/--end/--days/--every/--window/--workers/--root-dir/--data-dir/--out/--no-browser` all present at both `3458933` and `0b781b9`). Also: with a custom `--root-dir` the auto-parallel worker pool is disabled (`is_default_repo=False`) -> the run is **sequential**, slower but deterministic and identical in every strategy column.

**True A/B plan (owner runs, ~4 min):** replay the *older code* over the *same* pinned days -- `git worktree add /c/tmp/ab_3458933 3458933` (11/14 era, before `N1/N2/D1/D2/X`) and `/c/tmp/ab_0b781b9 0b781b9` (13/14 era, before `X1/X2/X3`), each `tools/raw_replay.py --dates 2026-11-03,...,2026-11-09 --every 60 --no-browser --out <file>`. Then a three-column delta (`3458933` | `0b781b9` | `73e2245`) on `[8b]`,`[8c]`,`[8d]`,`[8e]`,`[8h]`,`[8j]`,`[8k]` measures the accumulated work on one tape. **Run one at a time, never in the same window as the live loop.**

## Scalper quality verdict — `2026-11-10` (owner question: "can these 5 days prove the app makes money?")

**Honest answer: NO — and it is important to say it plainly.** The `11-03..11-09` tape proves two different things, and only one of them is what "profitable scalper" means.

**What the 5 days DO prove (all measured on `replay-36`, latest code):**
- **Safety and discipline:** every gate fires in the right order; `STEP 4` refused `100%` of cycles with confidence `< 35%`; the live ledger is honest (`6/6` cycles `SKIPPED`, flat, no orphan orders); the executor never guesses a position (`X1`), never discards a partial fill (`X2`) and never believes a close it has not verified (`X3`).
- **Real judges exist:** `value_area +413.4 pts (59.3% right)`, `vwap_trend +574.0 (52.7%)`, `l3_aggr_limit +25.1`; gates that block work too (`Late POWER Climax +54.5`, `n=15`).
- **Stability:** six same-tape replays produce identical `11-03..11-06` numbers, so the last seven stages moved nothing they should not have.

**What the 5 days DO NOT prove, with the numbers that say so:**
- **It is not profitable on this tape.** `[8d]` 1-position portfolio: **`9` trades, `5/4`, `55.6%` WR, `-21.8 pts`, PF `0.60`, MAX_DD `39.2`, POS_D `1/4`**. Every live exit ruler loses: `Stage-4a -188.3`, `Stage-4b PM -168.9`, `Legacy C3 -108.0`. Only the **dumb fixed bracket** is near break-even (`2.0x/3.5x = -2.1 pts`).
- **The sample is too small for a verdict.** `9` trades over `5` days, `1` positive day in `4`, `36` brackets. Law #5 asks for `3-5-10` days; a profit claim on `9` trades would be exactly the kind of fabricated certainty the house laws forbid. `PF 0.60` on `n=9` has a standard error wide enough to swallow a winning system.
- **It cannot answer "will it make money for us" without forward DEMO evidence** — which is what the live loop is now collecting, day by day, with the repaired executor.

**Therefore the sequencing:** (1) finish `14/14` (Evening Audit), (2) prove *improvement* on the raw tape by A/B (see `X-Ray & Manager Checkup`, commits `3458933` / `0b781b9` / `73e2245`), (3) build a **scalper quality scorecard** on a *bigger* pinned window (`10` days, `~30+` trades) with the fixed-bracket benchmark as the bar to beat, (4) only then discuss profit. **Tuning thresholds to manufacture trades before steps 1-3 would repeat every mistake the Book was written to prevent.**

---

## A/B RESULT — `2026-11-10` "how far did we come?" (three code eras, ONE tape, owner-run)

Both files authenticated before use: `ab_3458933.txt` has **no `[8k]`** and **no `Bell_Share`** (pre-`N1`/`N2`), `ab_0b781b9.txt` has **both** (post-`N1`/`N2`, pre-`X1/X2/X3`), both report `11-03..11-09` and `3,374` cycles / `3,169` signals.

**Zero strategy change across all three eras.** Excluding the `Generated:` stamp and the `SEC` wall-clock column, the files differ by **`18` lines (`3458933` vs `0b781b9`) and `10` lines (`0b781b9` vs `73e2245`) -- and every one of those lines is the `SEC` column itself.** Identical in A = B = C:

`[8d]` Live PM portfolio | `9` trd / `5/4` / `55.6%` / `-21.8` / PF `0.60` / DD `39.2` / POS_D `1/4` | same | same |

**What this proves (and what it forbids me from claiming):**
1. `N1`, `N2`, `D1`, `D1a`, `D2`, `X1`, `X2`, `X3` are **provably strategy-neutral** on this tape -- they bought **safety, honesty and reporting**, and nothing else. `[8k]` and `Bell_Share` appear only in the newer files: reporting, not decisions.
2. The only *apparent* improvement ever observed (`replay-30`'s `N=33 / 8 trades / -40.2`) was **tape growth**, not code: with the complete `795`-cycle `11-09`, the oldest and newest commits both land on `36` / `9` / `-21.8`. Settled.
3. **The app's profit did not improve.** Three code eras, one verdict: `PF 0.60`, `-21.8 pts`, `POS_D 1/4`, and a **dumb fixed 2.0x/3.5x bracket that still beats every live exit ruler by `~167 pts`** (`-2.1` vs `-168.9`).

## Stage L1c — Step 2 "the leaving" (Two-Door Exit) — `2026-11-11`

### Sources

Van Tharp (1998): Exits > entries; three exits: disaster stop, trailing stop, profit target; never widen a stop. Institutional bracket/OCO: entry + stop-market + take-profit limit, atomic. Imkeller & Rogers (2014): two-stop beats trailing. Alvarez 10-yr: fixed bracket wins; 4x–5x > 2x–3x.

### Problem (`replay-36`, `11-03..11-09`)

Fixed bracket `2.0×SL / 3.5×TP` = **−2.1 pts** (N=36, 38.9% WR) — bar to beat. All live rulers lose more: Stage-4a −188.3, Stage-4b −168.9, Legacy C3 −108.0. `WAIT` +1.86 vs `GO` −0.32. Tight stops: −43.2 vs −98.8.

### Two-Door Exit design (Law #9 — entry untouched)

| Door | Rule | Purpose |
|------|------|---------|
| **1 — Disaster stop** | Entry −2.0×SL, stop-market | Cap loss at 1R |
| **2 — Breakeven** | At +0.75×SL, move stop to entry | Can't lose (owner: 0.75, not 1.0) |
| **3 — Partial profit** | 50% off at +2.0×SL (limit) | Lock in gains |
| **4 — Trailing runner** | 50% trails at 1.0×ATR below price | Let winners run |
| **5 — Time stop** | No +1.0×SL after 4 M3 bars (~12m) → close | No middle ground |

### Fit

M3 scalper: 4 bars = 12 min (`AI_PROMPT_BARS=4`). Reuses Escort wave trailing. No entry changes — only *how we leave*. Demo-only (`ALLOW_LIVE_TRADING=0`); never lower thresholds; fixed bracket stays the bar.
### Acceptance criteria

Pinned replay `--dates 2026-11-03..2026-11-09` beats −2.1 pts on N=36 ([8c]); suite green `738 passed`; one isolated stage patch (`--check`+`apply`+`cmp`); one loop restart after install.

### Five-year-old recap

The robot goes fishing (enters a trade). Fixed bracket pulls up — sometimes too late (loses), too early (big fish swims away). **Two-Door Exit:** (1) safety net — yank at −2. (2) free game — fish to +0.75, hook slides to "can't lose." (3) pocket half — at +2, reel in half and keep. (4) let rest run — half on loose leash. (5) clock — nothing in 12 minutes, pull up elsewhere. Alarm stays on, goalkeeper strict — we teach the hands when to let go.
### Check-in stamp — `2026-11-11`

Step 2 design recorded. Door 2 breakeven at +0.75×SL (owner decision). Two-Door Exit = Doors 1–5.

Replay `--dates` = comma-list NOT `..`; DayStop feeds `[8d]` only, never `[8c]`. Path-check caught both problems BEFORE any replay ran — slow-mode rule proven. **VERDICT `replay-37` (`2026-11-11`, N=36): LOSS — Stage-2E `-200.2 pts` (8/25, 24.2% WR) vs fixed-bracket bar `-2.1` (14/22, 38.9%);** old rows bit-identical to `replay-36` (-188.3/-168.9/-166.7/-108.0) so the tool is sound. Diagnosis: executed brackets Avg SL=10.66 (2.7x ATR 3.91) -> BE@0.75R=+8.0pts fires far, 45m gate@<1R cuts slow winners, partial@2R=+21.3 is BEYOND avg TP 19.03 => Doors 3+4 nearly unreachable; Two-Door degrades to bracket+early-BE+time-stop and LOSES to it. ### Step 2 code patch — written & tested (`2026-11-11`)
config.py: Door 2 BE `0.5→0.75`; Door 3 partial `1.0→2.0`; Door 4 trail `0.50→2.0` / `0.50→1.00`. position_manager.py: Door 5 TIME_STOP fires at `r_now < 1.0`. 2 files, `--check`+`apply`+`cmp` on worktree `636f0ba`. Suite green: 738 passed, 3 subtests. Patch: `nov11_step2_code_patch.patch`. Next: pinned replay `--dates 2026-11-03..2026-11-09` vs −2.1 pts ([8c]); one isolated stage + one loop restart.

### Open-position awareness (`2026-11-11`, design note)

Yes. **PositionManager** (`position_manager.py`) owns open-position state (`self._state`): adopts every bot position (magic 234000), tracks ticket/entry/SL/TP/BE-done/age/high-water/A2-burst, runs `_manage_one()` every cycle → Door 1–5 exits. Manual trades never touched.

**Who knows:** `main.py` calls `get_position_manager().manage(snapshot)` each cycle; `step4_mt5_execution.py` places entry-time structural SL/TP; `risk_manager.py` risk-gates open positions; `trade_guard.py` blocks new entries when caps hit.

**What each section gives:** Entry: structural SL/TP beyond zones/walls. In-trade: BE at +0.75R, partial at +2.0R (bank half), trail at 1.0×ATR, time stop if no +1.0R after 12m/45m. Risk: daily cap, loss-streak, bracket bar ([8c] = −2.1 pts on N=36). No blind spot: PositionManager state is source of truth; every cycle re-reads live MT5 positions.

Five-year-old recap: The robot writes down every fish on its line — hook, depth, time. Every part reads that notebook before acting. Door 1–5 decides when to pull up. Risk decides if we can cast again. Nobody forgets the fish.

### Exit reporting rule — every SL/TP move logged with reason (`2026-11-11`, new rule)

**Rule:** Every time "the leaving" (Door 1–5) moves SL or TP, closes part or all, it must write a report line: **what moved, old → new value, R-level at the time, which Door fired, why, and the market price/bid-ask when it happened.** No exit action is silent.

**Why:** Owner's real case (`2026-11-11`, owner-corrected) — open position, market arrived near the TP line at ~$16; the robot then **pulled the TP line itself up to ~$30**; market never touched the new TP, turned around, and hit the SL (which had moved up from ~$3 to ~$9.65) — half the near-banked profit lost. Without an exit report we can't tell which door moved SL/TP, at what R, or whether the move helped or hurt. The report must log the robot's OWN line moves — including a TP pulled out of reach.

**Implementation:** Add `exit_report_log()` in `position_manager.py` — one line per SL/TP modification or close, appended to the journal/log: `timestamp | ticket | action | old_sl→new_sl | old_tp→new_tp | r_now | door | reason | bid | ask`. Read-only on existing data; no threshold changes; no new trades created. **Code patch → one loop restart.**

**Keep it out test:** If the report adds only noise (no actionable insight after 30 days of demo), remove it — never keep code that doesn't make the app better.

---

## Exit Report Specification — Detailed (`2026-11-11`, new rule)

### Report format (one line per SL/TP modification or close)

```
timestamp | ticket | action | old_sl→new_sl | old_tp→new_tp | r_now | door | reason | bid | ask
```

### Field definitions

| Field | Meaning | Example |
|-------|---------|---------|
| `timestamp` | ISO-8601 UTC when the exit fired | `2026-11-11T14:32:05Z` |
| `ticket` | MT5 position ticket | `974999` |
| `action` | What changed: `BE`, `PARTIAL`, `TRAIL`, `TP_PULL`, `CLOSE`, `DISASTER`, `TIME_STOP` | `BE` |
| `old_sl→new_sl` | SL before → after (or `—` if unchanged) | `4018.20→4020.00` |
| `old_tp→new_tp` | TP before → after (or `—` if unchanged) | `4030.00→4026.00` |
| `r_now` | Profit in R-multiples at the moment | `+0.75R` |
| `door` | Which Door fired (1–5) | `Door 2` |
| `reason` | Human-readable why | `breakeven triggered at +0.75R` |
| `bid` | MT5 bid price at that moment | `4020.05` |
| `ask` | MT5 ask price at that moment | `4020.15` |

### Example report lines

- Door 2 breakeven: `2026-11-11T14:32:05Z | 974999 | BE | 4018.20→4020.00 | — | +0.75R | Door 2 | breakeven triggered at +0.75R | 4020.05 | 4020.15`
- Door 3 partial profit: `2026-11-11T14:45:12Z | 974999 | PARTIAL | — | 4030.00→4026.00 | +2.0R | Door 3 | banked half at +2.0R | 4025.95 | 4026.05`
- Door 4 trail: `2026-11-11T14:50:33Z | 974999 | TRAIL | 4020.00→4023.50 | — | +2.5R | Door 4 | trailing runner at 1.0×ATR | 4024.00 | 4024.10`
- Door 5 time stop: `2026-11-11T14:52:00Z | 974999 | TIME_STOP | — | — | +0.4R | Door 5 | no +1.0R after 12 min, close at market | 4020.10 | 4020.20`

### Where reports are written

Appended to the same journal/log that `log_decision()` uses. Read-only on existing data — no threshold changes, no new trades created. Every report line is immutable once written.

---

## Owner's Real Case Study — Why Exit Reports Matter (`2026-11-11`, owner-corrected)

### What happened (owner's account — this version is authoritative)

An open position was running in profit:

- **TP line** sat at ~**$16**; the market arrived right up to it — the fish was almost in the bucket
- **The robot pulled the TP line up to ~$30** before $16 was ever touched — the target moved out of reach
- **SL had been moved up from ~$3 to ~$9.65** along the way (stop protecting the run)
- **Market got away and back** — never touched the new $30 TP — and **touched the SL at ~$9.65**
- **Result: half the catch lost** — ~$16 was nearly banked; the trade closed at ~$9.65 instead

### What an exit report would have shown

Without exit reports, we can't tell:
1. Which action pulled the TP line from $16 to $30 — a TP-trailing rule, a partial-profit adjust, or a manual edit?
2. Which door moved the SL $3 → $9.65, and at what R-level did each move happen?
3. Did pulling TP out of reach cost us the $16 that was almost touched?
4. Was the raised SL a good rescue (banked $9.65) or too timid a lock?

With exit reports, future review shows:
- Every SL **and TP** modification with old → new values and who made it
- Which Door fired and at what R-level
- The market price (bid/ask) at the moment of each move
- A complete timeline: entry → TP pulled $16→$30 → SL at $9.65 → stopped there

This lets us answer: "Did moving the lines help us, or did it cost us?" — and adjust the Door parameters accordingly.

---

## Implementation Code — `exit_report_log()` (`2026-11-11`, code patch)

Add to `position_manager.py` in the `PositionManager` class:

```python
def exit_report_log(self, ticket, action, old_sl, new_sl, old_tp, new_tp,
                    r_now, door, reason, bid, ask) -> None:
    """Write one immutable report line per SL/TP modification or close.

    Format: timestamp | ticket | action | old_sl→new_sl | old_tp→new_tp |
            r_now | door | reason | bid | ask
    """
    import time as _time
    ts = _time.strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ", _time.gmtime())
    def _fmt(v):
        return "—" if v is None else f"{v:.2f}"
    line = (
        f"{ts} | {ticket} | {action} | "
        f"{_fmt(old_sl)}→{_fmt(new_sl)} | "
        f"{_fmt(old_tp)}→{_fmt(new_tp)} | "
        f"{r_now:+.2f}R | {door} | {reason} | "
        f"{_fmt(bid)} | {_fmt(ask)}"
    )
    self._journal({"exit_report": line, "ticket": ticket, "action": action,
                   "door": door, "r_now": r_now})
    logger.info("EXIT-REPORT: %s", line)
```

Call `exit_report_log()` from every place in `_manage_one()` where SL or TP is modified:
- Door 1 disaster stop (initial SL/TP set at entry)
- Door 2 breakeven (SL → entry)
- Door 3 partial profit (TP adjusted, volume halved)
- Door 4 trailing runner (SL trails up)
- Door 5 time stop (position closed at market)

No threshold changes. No new trades created. Read-only logging. **Code patch → one loop restart.**

### Five-year-old recap (updated)

The robot goes fishing. Every time it moves the hook (SL) or the bucket (TP), it writes down what it did and why — like a fishing log. If a fish almost got away, we can read the log and see: did we move the hook too fast? Did we pull up too early? The log helps us fish better next time. If the log is just scribbles that don't help, we throw it away and keep fishing.

## PositionManager Code Changes — Door 1–5 + Exit Reporting (`2026-11-11`)

### config.py changes

| Setting | Old | New | Door |
|---------|-----|-----|------|
| `PM_BE_TRIGGER_R` | `0.5` | `0.75` | Door 2 — breakeven at +0.75×SL |
| `PM_PARTIAL_TRIGGER_R` | `1.0` | `2.0` | Door 3 — partial profit at +2.0×SL |
| `PM_C3_EARLY_TRAIL_R` | `0.50` | `2.0` | Door 4 — trail starts after Door 3 |
| `PM_C3_TRAIL_ATR` | `0.50` | `1.00` | Door 4 — trail at 1.0×ATR |

### position_manager.py changes

**Door 5 — TIME_STOP condition changed:**

Before:
```python
progress = ((mid - entry) if is_buy else (entry - mid)) / abs(cur_tp - entry)
...
if (eff_time_stop > 0
        and age_min >= eff_time_stop
        and progress < config.PM_TIME_STOP_MIN_PROGRESS):
```

After:
```python
if (eff_time_stop > 0
        and age_min >= eff_time_stop
        and r_now < 1.0):
```

Door 5 now closes if the position hasn't earned at least +1.0R profit after the age limit (12 min for A2 burst, 45 min for M3), instead of checking progress fraction toward TP.

**Exit reporting — new function `exit_report_log()`:**

Added to `PositionManager` class. Called from every SL/TP modification point:
- Door 1: initial SL/TP set at entry
- Door 2: SL → entry at breakeven
- Door 3: TP adjusted, volume halved at partial profit
- Door 4: SL trails up behind price
- Door 5: position closed at market on time stop

1. Base = commit whose `HOLY_BOOK.md` blob equals the blob on owner's `origin` HEAD
2. `--check` on clean worktree of base commit
3. `apply` the patch
4. `cmp` — applied result must match patch exactly
5. `wc -l` stated up front for each changed file
6. Owner-side Book edits ride only in the next patch
7. Assert each changed file's blob against the base when recently patched

## Exit Report Scenarios — Detailed Walkthroughs (`2026-11-11`)

### Scenario A: Door 2 breakeven saves the trade

Position: BUY at 4020.00, initial SL at 4018.00 (2.0 pts risk = 1R)

1. Market moves up to 4021.50 (+0.75R)
2. **Door 2 fires:** SL moves from 4018.00 → 4020.00 (entry)
3. Report: `... | BE | 4018.00→4020.00 | — | +0.75R | Door 2 | breakeven triggered at +0.75R | 4021.45 | 4021.55`
4. Market reverses to 4019.50
5. SL at 4020.00 is not touched — trade still open, risk = 0
6. Result: Trade can't lose — Door 2 worked correctly

### Scenario B: owner's corrected case — robot pulled the TP line out of reach

Position: BUY at 4020.00, initial SL at 4017.00 (3.0 pts risk = 1R), TP at ~4036.00 (+$16)

1. Market arrives right at the TP line (~4036.00) — fish almost in the bucket
2. TP is **not** touched — instead the exit logic pulls the TP line itself up to ~4050.00 (+$30)
3. SL has meanwhile moved up from 4017.00 to ~4026.65 (+$9.65 protected)
4. Market turns around, never touches the new $30 TP
5. Market falls back and touches the raised SL at ~4026.65 — position closed
6. **Result: ~$16 nearly banked became ~$9.65 — half the catch lost**
7. **Without reports:** We can't tell which rule pulled TP from $16 to $30, or if that was right
8. **With reports:** One line per move — `TP_PULL | 4036.00→4050.00 | +5.3R | Door 3 | TP adjusted after partial | bid | ask` — future review shows whether moving the target out of reach ever pays or only gives profit back

### Scenario C: Door 5 time stop closes a dead trade

Position: BUY at 4020.00, SL at 4018.00, TP at 4027.00

1. Market sits flat at 4020.50 for 12 minutes
2. r_now = +0.25R (barely in profit)
3. Age = 12 minutes (A2 burst limit)
4. **Door 5 fires:** r_now < 1.0R → close at market
5. Report: `... | TIME_STOP | — | — | +0.25R | Door 5 | no +1.0R after 12 min, close at market | 4020.45 | 4020.55`
6. Result: Margin freed, mind clear, ready for next setup

### Scenario D: Door 3 partial profit + Door 4 trail (combined)

Position: BUY at 4020.00, 2 lots

1. Market reaches +2.0R (4024.00)
2. Door 3 fires: close 1 lot, TP on remaining lot moves to lock profit
3. Report: `... | PARTIAL | — | 4024.00→4022.00 | +2.0R | Door 3 | banked half at +2.0R | 4023.95 | 4024.05`
4. Door 4 trail on remaining lot: SL trails at 1.0×ATR
5. Market continues to +5.0R
6. Door 4 keeps trailing SL up
7. Market reverses, hits trailing SL
8. **Reports show:** Door 3 banked half, Door 4 trailed runner, final exit at profit
9. Result: Even though full TP wasn't hit, trade ended profitable

## How to Read Exit Reports for Future Review (`2026-11-11`)

### Questions the reports answer

1. **Which door moved my SL/TP?** → Look at the `door` field
2. **At what profit level (R) did it happen?** → Look at `r_now`
3. **What was the old vs new value?** → Look at `old_sl→new_sl` and `old_tp→new_tp`
4. **What was the market price at that moment?** → Look at `bid`/`ask`
5. **Did this move help or hurt?** → Compare against subsequent price action

### Review workflow (weekly, demo)

1. Export all exit reports from the journal for the week
2. Group by door: Door 2 (BE), Door 3 (partial), Door 4 (trail), Door 5 (time stop)
3. For each door, ask: "Did this move improve the outcome?"
4. If Door 4 trails are consistently cutting winners short → consider widening trail buffer
5. If Door 2 BE triggers too early → consider raising trigger from 0.75R
6. If Door 5 closes too many trades that later win → consider extending time limit
7. Record any parameter changes as a new isolated stage patch

---

## Code Walkthrough — Where Exit Reports Are Called (`2026-11-11`)

### In `position_manager.py`, `_manage_one()` method

The exit reporting calls are placed at these points (in order of execution within each cycle):

1. **Door 1 — Disaster stop:** After `compute_structural_stops()` at entry, or on first adoption
   - Called when initial SL/TP is set or tightened
   - `action = "DISASTER"`, `door = "Door 1"`

2. **Door 2 — Breakeven:** When `r_now >= PM_BE_TRIGGER_R` and `not be_done`
   - Called when SL moves to entry
   - `action = "BE"`, `door = "Door 2"`

3. **Door 3 — Partial profit:** When `r_now >= PM_PARTIAL_TRIGGER_R` and partial not yet taken
   - Called when half volume is closed, TP adjusted
   - `action = "PARTIAL"`, `door = "Door 3"`

4. **Door 4 — Trailing runner:** After Door 3, when price moves favorably
   - Called when SL trails up by 1.0×ATR
   - `action = "TRAIL"`, `door = "Door 4"`

5. **Door 5 — Time stop:** When age >= limit and `r_now < 1.0`
   - Called when position is closed at market
   - `action = "TIME_STOP"`, `door = "Door 5"`

Each call passes: ticket, action, old/new SL, old/new TP, r_now, door name, reason string, bid, ask.

## App Architecture — How Every Section Knows About Open Positions (`2026-11-11`)

### The 14 sections and their position awareness

| # | Section | Knows open positions? | What it does with that knowledge |
|---|---------|----------------------|----------------------------------|
| 1 | Feed Reader (STEP 1) | Indirectly | Reads market data; positions don't affect feed |
| 2 | Brain Room (STEP 2) | Indirectly | Analyzes market structure; doesn't manage positions |
| 3 | Judges Panel | Indirectly | Votes on entry/exit signals; doesn't close positions |
| 4 | Scout (door-finder) | Indirectly | Finds entry/exit doors; doesn't manage positions |
| 5 | POWER v2 | Indirectly | Strength scoring; doesn't close positions |
| 6 | Shooting (planner) | Indirectly | Draws entry/exit plans; never sends orders |
| 7 | Crowd v2 | Indirectly | Fair ruler inside Shooting; advisory only |
| 8 | Paper Simulator + Escort | Indirectly | Practice room + babysitter for open positions |
| 9 | A2 — Three Eyes | Indirectly | NOW button for entry timing |
| 10 | Support Organs (L3, News, Volatility) | Indirectly | Context data for decisions |
| 11 | Doorman (STEP 3) | Yes | AI referee — can block entry/exit |
| 12 | Risk / L4 (firefighter) | Yes | Risk gate — sees positions, can block new trades |
| 13 | Corridor + MT5 Executor (STEP 4) | Yes | Sends SL/TP modifications to MT5 |
| 14 | Recorder (STEP 5) | Yes | Logs all position activity, including exit reports |

### PositionManager — the single source of truth

`PositionManager` (`position_manager.py`) is the only component that:
- Adopts every open bot position (magic 234000)
- Tracks per-position state: ticket, entry, SL, TP, BE-done, age, high-water, A2-burst flag
- Runs `_manage_one()` every cycle → applies Door 1–5 exits
- Writes exit reports for every SL/TP modification

All other sections that need position info read from PositionManager state or MT5 directly.

### Information flow per cycle

1. Feed Reader produces market snapshot
2. Brain Room analyzes, Judges vote, Shooting plans
3. Doorman approves/blocks
4. PositionManager.adopt() picks up any new MT5 positions
5. PositionManager._manage_one() applies Door 1–5 to each open position
6. Exit reports written for every SL/TP change
7. MT5 Executor sends modifications to broker
8. Recorder logs everything (STEP 5)

## Door-by-Door Code Reference (`2026-11-11`)

### Door 1 — Disaster Stop

- **Config:** Entry −2.0×SL, stop-market, never widens
- **Code location:** `_manage_one()` — initial SL set at entry/adoption
- **Exit report:** `action="DISASTER"`, `door="Door 1"`
- **Purpose:** Cap loss at 1R — never let a trade lose more than planned

### Door 2 — Breakeven (owner: +0.75R)

- **Config:** `PM_BE_TRIGGER_R = 0.75`
- **Code location:** `_manage_one()` — when `r_now >= 0.75` and `not be_done`
- **Exit report:** `action="BE"`, `door="Door 2"`
- **Purpose:** Trade can't lose — SL moves to entry
- **Owner's case:** SL was raised ~$3→~$9.65 during the run; exit reports will show which door moved it and at what R.

### Door 3 — Partial Profit (+2.0R, 50%)

- **Config:** `PM_PARTIAL_TRIGGER_R = 2.0`, `PM_PARTIAL_FRACTION = 0.5`
- **Code location:** `_manage_one()` — when `r_now >= 2.0` and partial not yet taken
- **Exit report:** `action="PARTIAL"`, `door="Door 3"`
- **Purpose:** Lock in half the gains

### Door 4 — Trailing Runner (1.0×ATR)

- **Config:** `PM_C3_EARLY_TRAIL_R = 2.0` (starts after Door 3), `PM_C3_TRAIL_ATR = 1.00`
- **Code location:** `_manage_one()` — after Door 3, trails SL up
- **Exit report:** `action="TRAIL"`, `door="Door 4"`
- **Purpose:** Let winners run — in owner's case, the line moves (SL up to $9.65, TP pulled to $30) are exactly what reports must attribute, door by door.

### Door 5 — Time Stop (12 min A2, 45 min M3)

- **Config:** `PM_TIME_STOP_MINUTES = 180`, `PM_M3_TIME_STOP_MINUTES = 45`, `A2_BURST_TIME_STOP_MINUTES = 12`
- **Code location:** `_manage_one()` — when age >= limit and `r_now < 1.0`
- **Exit report:** `action="TIME_STOP"`, `door="Door 5"`
- **Purpose:** No middle ground — if not +1.0R after time limit, close

## Glossary — Exit Reporting Terms (`2026-11-11`)

| Term | Definition |
|------|------------|
| R (R-multiple) | Profit/loss in units of initial risk (1R = distance from entry to initial SL) |
| BE | Breakeven — SL moved to entry price, trade can't lose |
| Partial | Partial profit take — close portion of position, keep rest running |
| Trail | Trailing stop — SL moves up behind price as profit grows |
| Time stop | Close position after time limit without sufficient profit |
| r_now | Current profit in R-multiples at the moment of the exit action |
| old→new | The SL/TP value before → after the exit modification |
| bid/ask | MT5 bid/ask prices at the moment of the exit action |
| Immutable report | Exit report line that cannot be changed once written |
| Keep-it-out test | Remove a feature if it adds no actionable insight after 30 days |

---

## When to Remove Exit Reporting (`2026-11-11`, keep-it-out test)

**Remove `exit_report_log()` if, after 30 days of demo:**

- Reports produce no actionable insight (no parameter changes suggested)
- Reports are pure noise (duplicate, obvious, or irrelevant information)
- Reports slow down the loop (measurable latency increase)
- Owner decides the reports aren't useful

**Keep `exit_report_log()` if, after 30 days of demo:**

- Reports helped identify why the lines moved (owner's TP-pulled-to-$30 → SL-hit-at-$9.65 case)
- Reports led to a parameter change that improved results
- Reports are reviewed regularly (weekly) and inform decisions

**Decision point:** 30 days from first demo use of exit reporting. Owner decides keep or remove.

## Sync-Everywhere — Blob SHA Verification (`2026-11-10`, standing law)

### Why blob SHA, not eyeball

The workspace, owner PC, and GitHub must match at all times. File contents can look identical but differ by a single byte (line endings, encoding, invisible characters). Blob SHA is the only trustworthy verification.

### Commands to verify before and after every push

```bash
# Current commit
git rev-parse HEAD

# Book blob SHA (must match owner's origin HEAD)
git rev-parse HEAD:HOLY_BOOK.md

# Tree hash
git rev-parse HEAD^{tree}

# Files changed in last commit
git show --stat HEAD

# Full blob list for a specific file
git cat-file -p HEAD:config.py | sha256sum
git cat-file -p HEAD:position_manager.py | sha256sum
git cat-file -p HEAD:HOLY_BOOK.md | sha256sum
```

### After pushing to GitHub

Verify on GitHub web UI:
1. Commit sha matches local `git rev-parse HEAD`
2. Tree hash matches
3. File stats (+/− lines) match `git show --stat HEAD`
4. Blob SHAs for `config.py`, `position_manager.py`, `HOLY_BOOK.md` match local

### What to do if mismatch

- Do NOT force push
- Fetch origin: `git fetch origin`
- Compare: `git diff HEAD origin/shadow-only-review`
- Resolve locally, then push again

## Owner's Real Case — Step-by-Step Analysis (`2026-11-11`, owner-corrected version)

### The situation (as corrected by owner — this is the record of truth)

Open position in profit:
- Entry price: not specified
- Initial SL: ~$3 (risk = 1R), raised later to ~$9.65
- TP line: ~$16 — the market ARRIVED on it (fish almost in the bucket)
- **Before the market touched TP, the robot pulled the TP line itself up to ~$30** — the target moved out of reach
- Market got away and back: never touched the new $30 TP
- Market touched the raised SL at ~$9.65
- Result: half the near-banked profit lost (~$16 became ~$9.65)

### What exit reports would show

| Event | What happened | Exit report line would show |
|-------|--------------|----------------------------|
| Entry | Position opened | Door 1: initial SL/TP set |
| SL $3→$9.65 | A door trailed the stop up | TRAIL line: old→new SL, R-level, door, why |
| Market nears $16 TP | Nothing fires yet | (no line — nothing moved) |
| **TP $16→$30** | **The robot moved its own target** | **`TP_PULL` line: old→new TP, R-level, door, why, bid/ask** |
| Market reverses | Price falls from below $30 | — |
| SL hit at $9.65 | Position closed | final report line with close price + loss vs $16 TP |

### The key question

Which rule pulled TP from $16 to $30 while the market sat right on the old TP? Was pulling the target ever useful, or did it only turn a ~$16 bank into ~$9.65?

**Without exit reports:** We can't answer this. We know the lines moved but not when, by which rule, or at what R-level.

**With exit reports:** Every line move — SL **and TP** — is one log line with timestamp, old→new value, R-level, door, and why. The review answers with data, not memory.

### The lesson

Exit reporting turns "the fish was almost in the bucket at $16 and half of it got away and I don't know why" into "rule X pulled TP from $16 to $30 at +5.3R while price sat on the old TP; price never reached $30; SL at $9.65 took the trade out; the TP-pull cost ~$6.35." That's actionable — we test whether pulling a nearly-touched target ever pays, or whether a TP within reach must never be moved away. This is why `TP_PULL` is added to the action vocabulary.

## Exit Reporting — Implementation Steps in Code (`2026-11-11`, developer reference)

### Step 1: Add `exit_report_log()` method to `PositionManager`

File: `position_manager.py`, inside the `PositionManager` class

```python
def exit_report_log(self, ticket, action, old_sl, new_sl, old_tp, new_tp,
                    r_now, door, reason, bid, ask) -> None:
```

### Step 2: Call `exit_report_log()` at Door 1 — Disaster Stop

Where initial SL/TP is set or tightened in `_manage_one()`:
```python
self.exit_report_log(
    ticket=ticket, action="DISASTER", old_sl=old_sl, new_sl=new_sl,
    old_tp=old_tp, new_tp=new_tp, r_now=r_now, door="Door 1",
    reason="initial structural SL/TP at entry", bid=bid, ask=ask
)
```

### Step 3: Call `exit_report_log()` at Door 2 — Breakeven

When `r_now >= PM_BE_TRIGGER_R` (0.75) and `not be_done`:
```python
self.exit_report_log(
    ticket=ticket, action="BE", old_sl=cur_sl, new_sl=entry,
    old_tp=cur_tp, new_tp=cur_tp, r_now=r_now, door="Door 2",
    reason=f"breakeven triggered at +{r_now:.2f}R", bid=bid, ask=ask
)
```

### Step 4: Call `exit_report_log()` at Door 3 — Partial Profit

When `r_now >= PM_PARTIAL_TRIGGER_R` (2.0) and partial not yet taken:
```python
self.exit_report_log(
    ticket=ticket, action="PARTIAL", old_sl=cur_sl, new_sl=new_sl,
    old_tp=cur_tp, new_tp=new_tp, r_now=r_now, door="Door 3",
    reason=f"banked half at +{r_now:.2f}R", bid=bid, ask=ask
)
```

### Step 5: Call `exit_report_log()` at Door 4 — Trailing Runner

When price moves favorably after Door 3:
```python
self.exit_report_log(
    ticket=ticket, action="TRAIL", old_sl=cur_sl, new_sl=new_sl,
    old_tp=cur_tp, new_tp=cur_tp, r_now=r_now, door="Door 4",
    reason=f"trailing runner at 1.0×ATR, +{r_now:.2f}R", bid=bid, ask=ask
)
```

### Step 6: Call `exit_report_log()` at Door 5 — Time Stop

When age >= limit and `r_now < 1.0`:
```python
self.exit_report_log(
    ticket=ticket, action="TIME_STOP", old_sl=cur_sl, new_sl=cur_sl,
    old_tp=cur_tp, new_tp=cur_tp, r_now=r_now, door="Door 5",
    reason=f"no +1.0R after {eff_time_stop:.0f} min, close at market",
    bid=bid, ask=ask
)
```

### Step 7: Test and ship

1. Add the method and all 5 call sites
2. Run `$PY -m pytest -q` → expect `738 passed, 3 subtests`
3. Run pinned replay `--dates 2026-11-03..2026-11-09` → verify exit reports appear
4. Ship as isolated stage patch (`--check` + `apply` + `cmp`)
5. **One loop restart** after install
6. Review after 30 days of demo — keep or remove per keep-it-out test

## Step 2d — POWER x MTF ENTRY (`2026-11-11`, owner-directed: "keep this topic in the Book and the to-do")

**Why (tape, `replay-37`):** `[9]` GO n=36 −0.06 pts/sig vs SKIPPED-WAIT n=57 **+1.86** — the waiting hand already knows more than the shooting hand. `[8b]` 51/57 waits = `POWER v2 says NEITHER`: the gate has no opinion because it reads only ONE chart (M3, `AI_PROMPT_BARS=4`) — it lacks information, not courage. GO is worst at +3m (−0.48) and +6m (−0.67), recovers after +9m: M3 confirms late → we enter at M3-exhaustion. Owner design call: give POWER 2–3 timeframes.

**Phase 0 — measurement (judge-side ONLY; `tools/raw_replay.py` new section `[8j]`, read-only, ship-side untouched; same pinned dates):**
- Per historical signal: sign(M15 trend/MA stack), M15 nearest structural wall vs own TP, sign(M3) agreement, M1 range/ATR ratio to M3, plus each team's per-TF score.
- Buckets: `M15 x M3` agree/oppose grid, `wall-ahead < 1.2xATR` vs clear, M1 range 0.5/1.0/2.0 x M3 — forward pts at +3/+6/+9/+12/+21/+45 min per bucket, n shown.
- Deliverable = the table that decides the design. No gate built from anything not in this table. `[8j]` text only, no browser-report change.

**Phase 1 — gate spec (written AFTER grid exists, thresholds fixed BEFORE first score):**
- M3 stays the SHOOTER. M15 = REFEREE: direction vote + wall-ahead veto. M1 = RULER, not judge: sizes fence/doors/tick-entry; a directional M1 vote allowed only if the grid proves it.
- `POWER v2` keeps Law #11: per-TF scores computed separately, no negotiation, AI never re-weights; veto power lives in the gate table, never in the AI prompt.
- Grid outcomes -> pre-registered rules, e.g. both signs agree -> GO; referee opposes -> WAIT; `NEITHER` + referee clear -> measured (maybe GO with small size). `NEITHER` must get a real meaning instead of a shrug.
- Mirror the gate as an `[8m]` scoreboard row (episode-deduped); bar = new `GO` cohort must beat old-gate GO on the SAME signals **and on both day windows (3d and 5d)**. Phase-1 first move = `[8m]` WRONGFUL-VETO RE-SCORE (episode-deduped) of the blocked cohorts, offline only.

**Phase 2 — sizing (M1 scalper geometry):** fence from M1 noise (1.0xATR_M1 ~ 2.5 pts on this week's sea) + wall shelter; doors sized in ATR units of the SAME TF as the fence — Step 2 autopsy law: **no door trigger beyond own TP; no teleport inside 1xATR of entry**. Ship order: gate scores win offline first, sizing second — never bundled.

**Acceptance bar (pre-registered, never bent):** the new entry must beat old POWER v2 on the same signals over the same pinned tape at n>=30; else the old entry stays. First ship touch only after Phase 0 numbers exist AND a green shadow score exists.

**Phase 0 RESULT (`replay-38`, `2026-11-11`, 3 pinned days 11-05/06/09, tool `feda784`/`5d210d23…`, rig=GitHub=workspace by hash):** MTF grids N=69/69 actionable (GO 30, WAIT 39; 36 waits still `POWER v2 says NEITHER`). (a) **DIRECTION-REFEREE HYPOTHESIS = NULL on this tape:** in BOTH M12xM3 primary and M15xM3 comparison grids the agree cells are the worst (`M12 UP x M3 UP FIX -0.53`; `M12 DOWN x M3 FLAT -1.03`; oppose cells better: fwd +2.77/+2.93, n=6/9 thin); M1 x M3 audition: aligned M1 row = worst big cell (FIX -3.44, n=21), MIXED row best (+1.73 fwd, n=24). Verdict = *no evidence to build the multi-TF directional vote* — not proof it can never work; frozen, evidence-bound. (b) **REAL SUSPECT = GATE VETO BLOCKS:** `[2]` per-cycle means `Climax block +4.80/sig (n=9)`, `Non-Trend BUY guard +4.80 (n=6)`, `POWER Opposes +1.33 (n=9)` vs GO +1.30; `Dead-Zone -4.87` + `NEITHER -1.03` stay blocked. Cycle-counted = double-count → honest re-score needed (`[8m]` episode-dedup, next patch). (c) Wall hint: tight +6.26 FIX (n=4) = noise. (d) Sea sizes: Avg ATR M1=3.24 / M3=5.47 / M12=9.87 → Phase-2 scalper geometry grounded (M1 fence ~3 pts vs current avg 10.4). (e) 3-day champion bracket = +39.1 vs 5-day -2.1 = pure day-selection power → both-window rule (Hygiene #2).
**Phase 1 v2 RESULT (`replay-40`, `2026-11-11`, same 3 days, tool `4d5d3f01…`): Hygiene #6 PASSED — `[8l]`+`[8c]` byte-identical to `replay-39` (verified by `diff`). CORRECTLY-BLOCKED (negative EPs, keep): `Family Gate` -60.4 (165 EP), `Dead-Zone` -16.8, `Conf<35` -17.9, `3b-Conflict` -6.9, all three WAIT cohorts negative.
